# Proposed OEIS comments: A135231 to A183714

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A135231

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A135231>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10649](../papers/10501-11000/10649-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Chai Wah Wu_, Aug 31 2023: (Start)
> Equivalent conjecture: a(n) = A128209(n+1) for n>0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 03 2026

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A135582

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A135582>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11609](../papers/11501-12000/11609-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (-n+1)*a(n) +6*(n-2)*a(n-1) +(-7*n+19)*a(n-2) +(-7*n+13)*a(n-3) +(13*n-31)*a(n-4) +2*(-n+4)*a(n-5) +4*(-2*n+5)*a(n-6)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 23 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

J. Mathar_, Jan 23 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 13, so the recurrence holds for n > 13. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

## A138164

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A138164>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [11994](../papers/11501-12000/11994-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: -8*n*(n-1)*a(n) + 12*(n+3)*(n-1)*a(n-1) + 2*(89*n^2-512*n+651)*a(n-2) + 3*(-127*n^2+715*n-966)*a(n-3) + 3*(-247*n^2+2431*n-5698)*a(n-4) + 9*(75*n-341)*(3*n-16)*a(n-5) - 72*(3*n-14)*(3*n-16)*a(n-6) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 24 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A140901

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A140901>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [6902](../papers/06501-07000/06902-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> (Empirical) Set p,q,r to n,5,3 (in any order) in s=p+q+r-1; a(n) = Product_{i=0..r-1} (binomial(s,p+i)*i!/(s-i)^(r-i-1)).
> (Conjecture) G.f.: (1 + 40*x + 400*x^2 + 1456*x^3 + 2212*x^4 + 1456*x^5 + 400*x^6 + 40*x^7 + x^8)/(1-x)^16. - _Bruno Berselli_, May 08 2012

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical formula is true. Reverse the rows and the columns: a matrix over 0..n that is nondecreasing along rows and down columns becomes one that is nonincreasing, which is exactly a plane partition with parts at most n inside a P X Q rectangle -- so a(n) counts the plane partitions in a P X Q X n box, which is the formula already noted on this entry. That triple product telescopes in its third index, leaving a(n) = Product_{i=1..P} Product_{j=1..Q} (n+i+j-1)/(i+j-1), a polynomial in n of degree P*Q. Clearing the denominator of the empirical expression then turns it into an identity between two polynomials whose degrees are bounded by inspection, so checking it at that many points settles it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3632](../papers/03501-04000/03632-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> (empirical) set p,q,r to n,11,10 (in any order) in s=p+q+r-1; a(n) = product {i in 0..r-1} (binomial(s,p+i)*i!/(s-i)^(r-i-1))

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical formula is true. Reverse the rows and the columns: a matrix over 0..n that is nondecreasing along rows and down columns becomes one that is nonincreasing, which is exactly a plane partition with parts at most n inside a P X Q rectangle -- so a(n) counts the plane partitions in a P X Q X n box, which is the formula already noted on this entry. That triple product telescopes in its third index, leaving a(n) = Product_{i=1..P} Product_{j=1..Q} (n+i+j-1)/(i+j-1), a polynomial in n of degree P*Q. Clearing the denominator of the empirical expression then turns it into an identity between two polynomials whose degrees are bounded by inspection, so checking it at that many points settles it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A141135

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A141135>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [990](../papers/00501-01000/00990-DISPROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Apr 05 2019: (Start)
> Conjecture: if n is a term in A121149, a(n) = a(n-1) + 3, otherwise a(n) = a(n-1) + 4. - _Jinyuan Wang_, Apr 05 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Both of these conjectures are false, and the entry's own terms refute them. The generating function reproduces a(1)..a(23) and then disagrees at n = 24, 27 and 30; the recurrence holds up to n = 23 and fails at n = 24 and again at n = 25. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A141353

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A141353>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11780](../papers/11501-12000/11780-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) + 2*(-4*n+1)*a(n-1) + 4*(5*n-7)*a(n-2) + 8*(-2*n+5)*a(n-3) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 15 2012

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A143017

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A143017>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [11995](../papers/11501-12000/11995-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: -8*n*(n+1)*a(n) + 4*n*(2*n+5)*a(n-1) + 4*n*(n+7)*a(n-2) + 2*(70*n^2-395*n+564)*a(n-3) + 2*(25*n^2-143*n+222)*a(n-4) + 4*(49*n-228)*(n-5)*a(n-5) - 45*(n-5)*(n-6)*a(n-6) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 14 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A155543

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A155543>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [120](../papers/00001-00500/00120-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) = A090129(2*n+2).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This identity is true. Both sides have a generating function that the entries themselves record, and after clearing denominators the difference is identically zero, which is a finite polynomial check. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

## A158690

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A158690>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [40](../papers/00001-00500/00040-PROOF.pdf), [41](../papers/00001-00500/00041-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures:
> Conjectural g.f.: G(exp(t)) as a formal power series in t, where G(q) := Sum_{n >= 0} q^(2*n+1) * Product_{k = 1..2*n} (1 - q^k). - _Peter Bala_, May 16 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, for the sequence and for every shift of it. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; the operator (1 + t) d/dt carries that property to every shift, and integrality then reduces a_i(n) mod p^r to an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, for which the Gauss congruences are classical. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A162314

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A162314>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [42](../papers/00001-00500/00042-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: for fixed k = 1,2,..., the sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the exact period dividing phi(k), where phi(k) is the Euler totient function A000010. For example, modulo 24 the sequence becomes [1, 4, 0, 16, 0 16, 0, 16, ...] with an apparent period of 2 beginning at a(2). - _Peter Bala_, Jul 08 2022
> Conjectural o.g.f. as a continued fraction of Stieltjes type: 1/(1 - 4*x/(1 - 2*x/(1 - 8*x/(1 - 4*x/(1 - ... - 3*n*x/(1 - 2*n*x/(1 - ...))))))). (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A167137

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A167137>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [43](../papers/00001-00500/00043-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> CONJECTURE: Sum_{n>=0} a(n)^m * log(1+x)^n/n! is an integer series in x for all integer m>0; see A167138 and A167139 for examples.
> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 16 we obtain the sequence [1, 1, 5, 15, 1, 7, 1, 7, 1, 7, ...], with an apparent period of 2 beginning at a(4).
> More generally, we conjecture that the same property holds for integer sequences having an e.g.f. of the form G(exp(x) - 1), where G(x) is an integral power series. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A172025

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A172025>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [9547](../papers/09501-10000/09547-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 2*n*(n+3)*a(n) + (-7*n^2 - 17*n - 8)*a(n-1) -2*(n+2)*(2*n+1)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 19 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The entry posts no generating function, but its definition a(n) = [x^n] f(x)g(x)^n gives one: reading the extraction as a contour integral and summing the geometric series leaves a single integral whose only pole inside a small circle is the branch of x = t*g(x) through the origin, and the residue there is an algebraic function. The recurrence then follows from it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A180752

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A180752>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8945](../papers/08501-09000/08945-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-2) - a(n-3) - a(n-5).
> Empirical: G.f.: -x^2*(-1+x+x^3+x^2) / ( (x-1)*(1+x)*(x^3+2*x-1) ). a(n)-a(n-2) = A052980(n-3). - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 24 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Every cell the condition names lies a bounded number of lines away -- two of them, where it reaches a knight's move -- so a window of that width decides the condition on the line in the middle, and the lines with nothing far enough beyond them are decided at the end. Counting a line at a time therefore gives a transfer matrix with 7 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A181207

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A181207>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9229](../papers/09001-09500/09229-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 15*a(n-3) + 9*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(8 - 15*x - 6*x^2 + 9*x^3) / ((1 - 5*x + 3*x^2)*(1 - 3*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 26 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A181255

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A181255>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [8374](../papers/08001-08500/08374-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> The number of 1s in each row repeats with period 3, and we can divide the matrices into 12 classes (013, 022, 031, 103, 112, 121, 130, 202, 211, 220, 301, or 310) based on the pattern of row sums. The number of matrices in each class satisfies b(n) = 3*b(n-1), 3*b(n-3), or 9*b(n-3), depending on the number of 1s and 2s in the pattern. Therefore, the combined sequence satisfies [(T - 3I)(T^3 - 3I)(T^3 - 9I)](a)(n) = 0, where T is the right shift operator defined by T(a)(n) = a(n+1), and I is the identity operator. This is equivalent to the empirical formula for a(n) given below. - _David Radcliffe_, Jan 12 2023
> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 12*a(n-3) - 36*a(n-4) - 27*a(n-6) + 81*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: 6*x*(21 - 7*x - 17*x^2 - 291*x^3 + 45*x^4 + 99*x^5 + 756*x^6) / ((1 - 3*x)*(1 - 3*x^3)*(1 - 9*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 26 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is a condition on every subblock of a fixed size, so a bounded window of consecutive lines decides it, and counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 12 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183304

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183304>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [9344](../papers/09001-09500/09344-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1)+a(n-3).
> Empirical G.f.: x*(1+x)^2/(1-2*x-x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 23 2012

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183314

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183314>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9230](../papers/09001-09500/09230-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 3*a(n-2) - a(n-3) - a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 + 3*x - 2*x^2 - x^3) / (1 - x - 3*x^2 + x^3 + x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 27 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [8830](../papers/08501-09000/08830-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + a(n-2) + 2*a(n-3) - a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 + x)^2*(1 + x - x^2) / (1 - x - x^2 - 2*x^3 + x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 27 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 57 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183355

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183355>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9542](../papers/09501-10000/09542-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n)=3*a(n-1) for n>2

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 3 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183356

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183356>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10576](../papers/10501-11000/10576-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 2*a(n-2) - a(n-3) for n>4.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 28 2018: (Start)
> Assuming Colin Barker's conjectures, a(n) = (12*Fibonacci(n+1))^2, n>1. - _Ehren Metcalfe_, Apr 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183452

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183452>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [1355](../papers/01001-01500/01355-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: g.f. -x*(1 +5*x +607360*x^20 -11532*x^9 +753306*x^19 +6*x^2 -288*x^26 -57080*x^10 -612804*x^16 +7234*x^8 -600857*x^17 +181998*x^18 -57*x^3 -10592*x^25 -16800*x^24 -182104*x^23 -283*x^4 +5942*x^7 -132231*x^15 -345*x^5 +1200*x^6+ 285093*x^14 +259034*x^13 +52803*x^12 +384*x^29 +768*x^28 +2048*x^27 -67623*x^11 -344072*x^22 -99176*x^21)/(-1 +x +275562*x^20 +15867*x^9 -37876*x^19 +x^2 -8768*x^26 +384*x^30 -7896*x^10 -20075*x^16 +1261*x^8 -184239*x^17 -567185*x^18 +60*x^3 -8096*x^25 -174456*x^24 -168944*x^23 -52*x^4+980*x^7 +311194*x^15-58*x^5 -1393*x^6 +65529*x^14 +24304*x^13 -94858*x^12 +384*x^29 +1664*x^28 -1952*x^27 -12862*x^11 +73800*x^22 +537480*x^21) . - _R. J. Mathar_, Dec 03 2022

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 1 056 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183547

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183547>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [7689](../papers/07501-08000/07689-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 3*a(n-2) + 4*a(n-3) - 9*a(n-4) - 13*a(n-6) + 8*a(n-7) + 4*a(n-8) + 7*a(n-9) + 2*a(n-10).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 + 5*x^2 - 23*x^3 + 10*x^4 - 32*x^5 + 17*x^6 + 13*x^7 + 9*x^8 + 10*x^9) / (1 - 5*x + 3*x^2 - 4*x^3 + 9*x^4 + 13*x^6 - 8*x^7 - 4*x^8 - 7*x^9 - 2*x^10). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 29 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Every cell the condition names lies a bounded number of lines away -- two of them, where it reaches a knight's move -- so a window of that width decides the condition on the line in the middle, and the lines with nothing far enough beyond them are decided at the end. Counting a line at a time therefore gives a transfer matrix with 13 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A183587

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183587>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9231](../papers/09001-09500/09231-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 24*a(n-1) - 30*a(n-2) - 296*a(n-3) + 192*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(56 - 101*x - 772*x^2 + 512*x^3) / (1 - 24*x + 30*x^2 + 296*x^3 - 192*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 29 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 4 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [8375](../papers/08001-08500/08375-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 12*a(n-1) + 20*a(n-2) - 238*a(n-3) - 420*a(n-4) + 324*a(n-5) + 400*a(n-6) - 224*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(120 + 39*x - 2974*x^2 - 5338*x^3 + 4094*x^4 + 5400*x^5 - 3024*x^6) / (1 - 12*x - 20*x^2 + 238*x^3 + 420*x^4 - 324*x^5 - 400*x^6 + 224*x^7). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 30 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 10 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A183645

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A183645>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2850](../papers/02501-03000/02850-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 125 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [7527](../papers/07501-08000/07527-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) + 5*a(n-2) - 42*a(n-3) - 2*a(n-4) + 84*a(n-5) - 20*a(n-6) - 46*a(n-7) + 19*a(n-8) + 2*a(n-9) - a(n-10).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 3 126 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
